\mathsf{Ising}
symmetry,

\phi:~\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})\to{\mathcal{S}}(\mathfrak{B}^{\prime})\,,

(C.66)

where

is the multi-fusion category formed by topological line operators living on the boundary
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
.

e_{RGB}
. The former leads to a phase where a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subsymmetry of
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
is spontaneously broken in both vacua. In the latter case, one finds that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subsymmetry of
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
is spontaneously preserved in both vacua. Thus for gapped (or gapless) phases we would call these in the former case
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phases and in the latter case
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})
SSB phases.

By looking at the linking action of the
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
generators, one can deduce their images on the collapsed sandwich as

\displaystyle\phi(1)

\displaystyle=1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}

(C.79)

\displaystyle\phi(R)

\displaystyle=1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}

\displaystyle\phi(G)

\displaystyle=1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}

\displaystyle\phi(RG)

\displaystyle=1_{00}\oplus 1_{11}

\displaystyle\phi(B)

\displaystyle=2(1_{01}\oplus 1_{10})\,,

which is again consistent with
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
fusion rules and e.g. the homomorphism
1\rightarrow(1_{00}\oplus 1_{11})
,
P\rightarrow(1_{01}\oplus 1_{10})
when compared to (
C.30
).

We can now see there is a clear difference between this
n=2
SSB phase and the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase seen above. In the previous phase, it was
\phi(R)(=\phi(G))
that acted to exchange the vacua, which was the spontaneously broken
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subsymmetry of
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
. Now
\phi(R)=\phi(G)=1
and instead it is
\phi(B)
that exchanges the vacua but also in the process multiplies them by a factor of 2, i.e.

B:\quad v_{0}\rightarrow 2v_{1},\qquad v_{1}\rightarrow 2v_{0}\,.

(C.80)

As the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subsymmetry is preserved in this phase, we can describe this phase as a
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})
SSB phase.

Mathematically, we have provided information on another pivotal tensor functor describing a different
n=2
SSB phase, in this case the
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})
SSB phase,

\phi:~\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})\to\text{Mat}_{2}(\mathsf{Vec})\,,

(C.81)

where
\text{Mat}_{2}(\mathsf{Vec})
is the multi-fusion category formed by
2\times 2
matrices in
\mathsf{Vec}
with no non-trivial relative Euler terms.

. One instead finds that the two original vacua now split into two each as

v_{0}^{\pm}=\frac{v_{0}\pm\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{1}}{2}\,,\qquad v_{1}^{\pm}=\frac{v_{1}\pm\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{2}}{2}\,,

(C.86)

which means we further break down another
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subsymmetry of
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
in both original vacua and thus produce 4 vacua in total which are exchanged by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
spontaneously broken subsymmetry of
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
. The originally broken
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
exchanged the vacua
v_{0}\leftrightarrow v_{1}
and now it is enlarged as it exchanges
v_{0}^{+}\leftrightarrow v_{1}^{+}
and
v_{0}^{-}\leftrightarrow v_{1}^{-}
.

One can then conclude that all three ways of sequentially condensing various algebras ultimately lead to the same result. SSB phase.

{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{1}}

(C.90)

One finds that after collapsing the sandwich all
{\mathcal{E}}
’s become local operators related to the original operators in (
C.61
) where
{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{i}}
descend to operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{i}}
descend to
{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
which are the collapsed operators
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
, whereas the original end
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
will not end on the right and thus not descend to a non-trivial local operator. The fusion rules for the new set of local operators
\{1,{\mathcal{O}}_{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{2},{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\}
will then follow the one in (
C.61
) with
{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\sim{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
. The new set of local operators now follows the fusion rules

\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}_{1}^{2}&=1,\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}&={\mathcal{O}}_{2},\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{i}&={\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{i},\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{2}^{2}&=(1+{\mathcal{O}}_{1})/2,\\
\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{O}}_{2}{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}&={\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2},\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{2}{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}&={\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1},\\
({\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{i})^{2}&=(1+{\mathcal{O}}_{1})/2,\\
{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}&={\mathcal{O}}_{2}.\\
\end{aligned}

(C.91)

Now we can again form more canonical-looking operators by defining

\begin{aligned} v_{0}&=\frac{1-{\mathcal{O}}_{1}}{2},\\
v_{1}&=\frac{1+{\mathcal{O}}_{1}+2{\mathcal{O}}_{2}}{4},\\
v_{2}&=\frac{1+{\mathcal{O}}_{1}-2{\mathcal{O}}_{2}}{4},\\
\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} &\\
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{1}&=\frac{{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}+{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}}{2}\,,\\
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{2}&=\frac{{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}-{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}}{2}\,,\\
\end{aligned}

(C.92)

with this redefinition one finds

v_{a}v_{b}=\delta_{ab}v_{b},\quad v_{a}\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{i}=\delta_{ai}\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{i},\quad\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{j}=\delta_{ij}v_{j},

(C.93)

for
a,b\in\{0,1,2\}
and
i,j\in\{1,2\}
.

As the original end of the
m
line on
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
denoted by
\widehat{{\mathcal{E}}}_{0}
is now trivial, one finds that the vacuum
v_{0}
remains a good vacuum. However, as the original ends of the two
e
lines
\widehat{{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}
and
\widehat{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2}
now become local operators
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
and
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
after collapsing the sandwich, the original vacua
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
both split in two, so one finds a new set of vacua

\displaystyle v_{0}=v_{0}\,,\qquad v_{1}^{\pm}=\frac{v_{1}\pm\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{1}}{2}\,,\qquad v_{2}^{\pm}=\frac{v_{2}\pm\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}{2}\,.

(C.94)

However for our purposes, it may be easier to take these vacua and relabel them as

v_{1}=v_{1}^{+}\,,\quad v_{R}=v_{2}^{+}\,,\quad v_{G}=v_{2}^{-}\,,\quad v_{RG}=v_{1}^{-}\,,\quad v_{B}=v_{0}\,,

(C.95)

as this will make it easier the effect of the linking actions of
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
on these vacua

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle:\quad v_{B}\rightarrow v_{B}\,,\qquad v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{R}\,,\qquad v_{G}\leftrightarrow v_{RG}\,,

(C.96)

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle:\quad v_{B}\rightarrow v_{B}\,,\qquad v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{G}\,,\qquad v_{R}\leftrightarrow v_{RG}\,,

\displaystyle RG

\displaystyle:\quad v_{B}\rightarrow v_{B}\,,\qquad v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{RG}\,,\qquad v_{R}\leftrightarrow v_{G}\,,

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle:\quad v_{B}\rightarrow v_{1}+v_{R}+v_{G}+v_{RG}\,,\quad v_{1},v_{R},v_{G},v_{RG}\rightarrow v_{B}\,,

from which we can see that the vacua indeed transform among themselves according to the broken
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
symmetry, with no non-trivial relative Euler terms between the vacua. From these linking actions of the
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
generators, one can deduce their images on the collapsed sandwich as

\displaystyle\phi(1)

\displaystyle=1_{BB}\oplus 1_{11}\oplus 1_{RR}\oplus 1_{GG}\oplus 1_{(RG)(RG)}

(C.97)

\displaystyle\phi(R)

\displaystyle=1_{BB}\oplus 1_{1R}\oplus 1_{R1}\oplus 1_{G(RG)}\oplus 1_{(RG)G}

\displaystyle\phi(G)

\displaystyle=1_{BB}\oplus 1_{1G}\oplus 1_{G1}\oplus 1_{R(RG)}\oplus 1_{(RG)R}

\displaystyle\phi(RG)

\displaystyle=1_{BB}\oplus 1_{1(RG)}\oplus 1_{(RG)1}\oplus 1_{RG}\oplus 1_{GR}

\displaystyle\phi(B)

\displaystyle=1_{B1}\oplus 1_{BR}\oplus 1_{BG}\oplus 1_{B(RG)}

\displaystyle\oplus 1_{1B}\oplus 1_{RB}\oplus 1_{GB}\oplus 1_{(RG)B}\,,

which is again consistent with
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
fusion rules.

Mathematically, we have provided information on a pivotal tensor functor describing a full
n=5
SSB

\phi:~\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})\to\text{Mat}_{5}(\mathsf{Vec})\,,

(C.98)

where
\text{Mat}_{5}(\mathsf{Vec})
is the multi-fusion category formed by
5\times 5
matrices in
\mathsf{Vec}
with no non-trivial relative Euler terms. We refer to a phase with such symmetry structure as a
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
SSB phase.

Appendix D
Kennedy-Tasaki (KT) transformations for
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
and phase transitions

In this Appendix, we discuss a few representative examples of Kennedy-Tasaki (KT) transformations for theories with
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
symmetry using the SymTFT Quiches found in the preceding Appendix. These examples provide further details on the results shown in Table
4
, Table
5
and Table
6
.

The maps constructed below are inspired by the
Kennedy–Tasaki (KT) unitary
[
179
]
,
which converts the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
Haldane SPT into a symmetry-broken phase with the symmetry. Here we adopt the term in a broader sense:
our “generalized KT” transformations, introduced in
[
180
,
10
]
, realize a categorical analogue of the original KT duality of the Haldane chain.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}
denote the target symmetry category and let

{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{A}}
be the gapped interface determined by a condensable algebra
{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}})
.
Collapsing the two intervals in the diagram

\mathrm{SymTFT}({\mathcal{S}})\;\xleftarrow{\;{\mathcal{I}}_{{\mathcal{A}}}\;}\;\mathrm{SymTFT}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})\;\dashrightarrow\;\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}

implements a functorial map

{\mathcal{K}}^{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}:\;\text{QFT}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}\;\longrightarrow\;\text{QFT}_{{\mathcal{S}}},

(D.1)

which sends any
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric phase – gapped or gapless – to an

{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric one.
On the level of topological lines this map is the charge-conversion functor

F_{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}\!:\;\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})\;\longrightarrow\;\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}}),\qquad F_{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}\bigl(\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{a}\,n_{a,a^{\prime}}\,\mathbf{Q}_{a},

(D.2)

where the integers
n_{a,a^{\prime}}
are the multiplicities appearing in the
minimal Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}_{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}=\bigoplus_{a,a^{\prime}}n_{a,a^{\prime}}\,\mathbf{Q}_{a}\overline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{S}})\boxtimes\overline{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})

that completes the interface.
Thus the transformation simply re-labels symmetry defects (and hence
phase data) without closing the gap, thereby reproducing the structural
role of the lattice KT unitary
[
179
]
in a fully topological
framework.

When
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}
is minimal, the map
{\mathcal{K}}^{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
preserves
irreducibility: it carries irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
-symmetric gapped phases
and critical points onto irreducible
{\mathcal{S}}
-symmetric ones.
Whether the resulting theory is gapped or gapless is dictated solely by the choice of physical boundary

\mathfrak{B}^{\text{phys}}
;
the KT transformation itself simply transports a known critical point (such
as the Ising CFT) between different symmetry categories.

Thus, generalized KT transformations are a powerful tool that allows one to construct a map from a theory with an
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
symmetry to a theory with a larger symmetry
{\mathcal{S}}
. The main use of such a tool can then be found in realizing new phase transition theories from ones which are known. A classic example of phase transition theory is the 2d Ising CFT

\mathfrak{T}^{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}_{C}=\mathsf{Ising}
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(D.3)

which serves the role of a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric phase transition. It turns out that for
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
symmetry one can effectively construct any phase transition using the Ising CFT or a few copies of it.

The Ising CFT has three operators of interest for our discussion which are listed in Table
8
:

Name

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-charge

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-sector

\sigma

spin/order

-1

untwisted

\mu

disorder

+1

twisted

\epsilon

energy

+1

untwisted

Table 8:
Operators in the Ising CFT

where a twisted-sector operator is attached to the line
P
generating
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry. If we take the SymTFT

\mathfrak{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})
with

\mathfrak{B}^{\text{sym}}_{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}=\mathcal{L}_{e}=1\oplus e,

(D.4)

then from table
8
, we deduce that the generalized charges are
[
10
]
:

q(\sigma)=e,\quad q(\mu)=m,\quad q(\epsilon)=1.

(D.5)

As is well-known, the Ising CFT describes a phase transition between an SPT phase for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry and a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SSB phase, which we denote by
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{\text{SPT}}
and
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{\text{SSB}}
respectively.
The former consists of a single vacuum on which
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is realized trivially

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{\text{SPT}}=\,
Trivial
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(D.6)

which has a string order parameter given by
\mu
,
whereas the latter presents two vacua exchanged by the spontaneously broken
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry generator

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}_{\text{SSB}}=\,
Trivial
{}_{0}\,\oplus\,
Trivial
1
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

(D.7)

We denote the identity local operators (also called vacua) in each theory as
v_{i},\,i\in\{0,1\}
, which satisfy

v_{i}v_{j}=\delta_{ij}v_{i},\qquad 1\equiv v_{0}+v_{1}.

(D.8)

The broken
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
generator
P
acts by exchanging the two vacua

P:\quad v_{0}\leftrightarrow v_{1}\,.

(D.9)

The order parameter for SSB of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry is the spin operator
\sigma
of the Ising CFT, which acquires a non-zero vacuum expectation value (vev) in each vacuum. It can be identified as the operator

\sigma\equiv v_{0}-v_{1}

(D.10)

where the coefficients are the vev that
\sigma
takes in each vacuum. We note that the action of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
generator
P
, eq. (
D.9
), sends
\sigma\to-\sigma
, as required.

D.1

\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
on the Ising CFT. Other phase transitions can be found analogously using the machinery found in
[
10
]
or using similar calculations in this Appendix. We also provide the KT transformation describing the igSPT with non-invertible symmetry which is one of the main heroes of this work which we note is not a phase transition as it can only flow to a single gapped phase.

Nevertheless, it is still interesting to study how operators map from a theory with anomalous group symmetry to a theory with non-invertible symmetry. It also allows one to combine two known/existing KT transformations to produce a new KT transformation which may not be known or easy to compute.

D.2
Condensable algebras of dimension 2

Here we provide a few examples of KT transformations from
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
as well as the one example of KT transformation from
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
to
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
. The input phase transition for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry is simply an
\text{Ising}\otimes\text{Ising}
CFT whereas for
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
it is the 4-State Potts model. Notably for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
case, we show the KT transformation describing the
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})
gSSB phase which can be used as a guide for finding other complicated phase transitions within the
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
system.

\displaystyle\to m_{GB}

\displaystyle m_{1},\,m_{2}

\displaystyle\to m_{B}

\displaystyle e_{1}m_{2},\,e_{2}m_{1}

\displaystyle\to m_{G}

\displaystyle\to f_{GB}

\displaystyle\to f_{B}

\displaystyle e_{1}m_{1},\,e_{2}m_{2}

\displaystyle\to f_{G}\,.

D.2.3
KT from
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
to
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
symmetry II

From the expression for the condensable algebra
3Condensable Algebras of

Z

(

Rep

(

D
8

)

)

with their quantum dimension, label and

S
′

. The associated Reduced Topological Order is

Z

(

)

. The last two columns describe the phases once the symmetry

=

(

)

is fixed and the number of universes

n

, defined in equation ().
in (
B.10
) we observe that the map

\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Vec}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}})\to\mathcal{Z}(\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8}))

(D.55)

of generalized charges is

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\to 1\oplus e_{RGB}

(D.56)

\displaystyle e_{2}m_{1}

\displaystyle\to e_{R}\oplus e_{GB}

\displaystyle e_{1}m_{2}

\displaystyle\to e_{G}\oplus e_{RB}

\displaystyle e_{1}e_{2}m_{1}m_{2}

\displaystyle\to e_{B}\oplus e_{RG}

\displaystyle e_{1},m_{2}

\displaystyle\to m_{RB}

\displaystyle e_{2},\,m_{1}

\displaystyle\to m_{GB}

\displaystyle e_{1}e_{2},\,m_{1}m_{2}

\displaystyle\to m_{RG}

\displaystyle e_{2}m_{1}m_{2},\,e_{1}e_{2}m_{1}

\displaystyle\to f_{RB}

\displaystyle e_{1}m_{1}m_{2},\,e_{1}e_{2}m_{2}

\displaystyle\to f_{GB}

\displaystyle e_{1}m_{1},\,e_{2}m_{2}

\displaystyle\to f_{RG}\,.

Now one can again write out all the operators and their maps, however, given we have already shown these for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
we will only show a few examples of dealing with some complications here while the rest can be computed similarly. The main difficulty here lies in the fact that the SymTFT Quiche will have a reducible symmetry boundary of the form

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}_{e}\oplus\mathfrak{B}_{m}

(D.57)

where
\mathfrak{B}_{m}=\mathfrak{B}_{e}/(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})
mixes two gauge-opposite irreducible boundaries of the SymTFT for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
symmetry, with a trivial relative Euler term between the two boundaries.

The
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
-symmetric, KT-transformed phase transition theory can be written in terms of an input
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
-symmetric theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}}
as

\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}}\oplus(\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2})
\rho
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})}\,\,=
\rho
B

(D.58)

where
\rho=1,R,G,RG\subset\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
. In fact, we can recognize
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}}
as a product of two copies of a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetric input theory
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
. For our minimal case, we can take
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
to be the 2d Ising CFT, and therefore our
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
input transition
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}}
will simply be
\text{Ising}\otimes\text{Ising}=\text{Ising}^{2}
.

Now we are in a position to discuss how operators of an input theory map to operators of the KT-transformed theory. For example:

•

An untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}}
uncharged under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to two operators: an untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
uncharged under
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
and an untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RGB}}
charged under
B\in\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
. These operators can be recognized as

{\mathcal{O}}_{1}=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{e}+({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{m}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RGB}}=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{e}-({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{m}\,,

(D.59)

where
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{e}
is a copy of
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{T}^{e}:=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}}
and
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{m}
is a copy of
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{T}^{m}:=\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
of (
D.58
).
This is consistent with the (passing-through) symmetry action seen in (
C.20
) as

S_{em}:\,({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{e}\to({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{m}\,,\quad S_{me}:\,({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{m}\to({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1})_{e}\,,

(D.60)

while
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{1}
is invariant under
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
, hence the operators
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RGB}}
are uncharged under
R
and
G
but under
\phi(B)=S_{em}\oplus S_{me}
one finds

\phi(B):\,{\mathcal{O}}_{1}\to 2{\mathcal{O}}_{1}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RGB}}\to-2{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RGB}}\,,

(D.61)

as expected.

•

A
P_{2}
-twisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}}
with charge (1,0) under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
descends to two operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})}
: a
G
-twisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{G}}
uncharged under
\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
and a
G
-twisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RB}}
charged under
B\in\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
. These operators can be recognized as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{G}}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{e}+({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{m}\,,

(D.62)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RB}}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{e}-({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{m}\,,

where
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{e}
is a copy of
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{1}m_{2}}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{T}^{e}
which is
(P_{2})_{ee}
-twisted and
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{m}
is a copy of
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{1}m_{2}}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{T}^{m}
which is
(P_{1})_{mm}
-twisted after gauging of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
symmetry. Both operators can thus be viewed as sitting at the end of
\phi(G)\supset(P_{2})_{ee},(P_{1})_{mm}
as required. This is consistent with the
B
symmetry action (and hence also the linking action) seen in (
C.20
) as

\displaystyle S_{em}

\displaystyle:\,({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{e}\to({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{m}\,,

(D.63)

\displaystyle S_{me}

\displaystyle:\,({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{m}\to({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}m_{2}})_{e}\,,

however, we also require
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{G}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RB}}
to be invariant under
\phi(R)
and
\phi(G)
, while in its current form it would seem they are both charged under
\phi(R)
and
\phi(RG)
. To remedy this, one chooses a particular non-trivial junction between the
\phi(G)
and
\phi(R)
lines to cancel this inconsistency. The presence of such a junction is then important for other operators in the theory to be charged correctly as we will see shortly with the
m_{RB}
multiplet.

Hence the operators
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{G}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RB}}
are then uncharged under
R
and
G
but under
\phi(B)=S_{em}\oplus S_{me}
one finds the linking action

\phi(B):\,{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{G}}\to 2{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{G}}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RB}}\to-2{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RB}}\,,

(D.64)

which is consistent with the results for
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RGB}}
above as
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RB}}={\mathcal{O}}_{e_{G}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RGB}}
.

•

Finally for a more complicated multiplet, we can pick the untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}}
of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}}
with charge (1,0) under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
which descends to two operators of
\mathfrak{T}^{\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})}
in the
m_{RB}
multiplet: an untwisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},1}^{e_{1}}
and a
G
-twisted operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},2}^{e_{1}}
both charged under
R,RG,B\in\mathsf{Rep}(D_{8})
. These operators can be recognized as

{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},1}^{e_{1}}=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}})_{e}\,,\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},2}^{e_{1}}=({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}})_{m}\,,

(D.65)

where
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}})_{e}
is a copy of
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{1}}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{T}^{e}
which untwisted and
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}})_{m}
is a copy of
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{1}}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{T}^{m}
which is
(P_{1})_{mm}
-twisted after gauging of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
symmetry. Thus
{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},2}^{e_{1}}
should be viewed as an operator living at the end of
\phi(G)\supset(P_{1})_{mm}
, while
{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},1}^{e_{1}}
is untwisted as required.

Both
{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},1}^{e_{1}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},2}^{e_{1}}
are charged under
R
but not
G
, while the symmetry action of
B
exchanges
{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},1}^{e_{1}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},2}^{e_{1}}
as
S_{em}:\,({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}})_{e}\to({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}})_{m}
and similarly for
({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{e_{1}})_{m}
. Note that the charge of
{\mathcal{O}}_{m_{RB},2}^{e_{1}}
under
R
can be obtained from a choice of a non-trivial junction operator between the symmetry line
\phi(R)
and twist line
\phi(G)
as was the case for
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{G}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{e_{RB}}
.

Other examples can be computed similarly.
